# Kindhearted Art-loving Reader!

After I translated various small treatises on the **Philosopher's Stone** <note>original: "Stein der Weißen"; the legendary substance capable of turning base metals into gold and granting eternal life</note> from Latin into the High German language a few years ago and saw them through to print, I have now noticed that such work is not unpleasing to the lovers of Hermetic Philosophy <note>The esoteric tradition based on the writings attributed to Hermes Trismegistus</note> in Germany, especially those who are not skilled in the Latin language. Therefore, at the request of good friends, I have translated several more of the same into the High German language and brought them together in this little book, to which I have given the name **Alchemical Seven-Stars** <note>original: "Alchymiſtiſch Sieben-Geſtirn"; likely referring to the Pleiades or the seven classical planets/metals</note>, for the reason that the mariners in these lands guide themselves by the Seven-Stars so that they may steer their course rightly and into the desired haven

<meta>catchword: ven</meta>

<summary>The author introduces a collection of alchemical texts translated into the vernacular to assist German-speaking seekers in navigating the complexities of the "Philosopher's Stone."</summary>
<keywords>Philosopher's Stone, Hermetic Philosophy, Alchemical Seven-Stars, translation, alchemy, navigation</keywords>

may come to pass: Thus I hope that this little book will likewise be able to show those eager for the highly noble art of <term>Alchemy<note>original: Alchymiæ</note></term> a correct path to eventually reach their desired goal. For although I am not a <term>practitioner<note>original: Practicus; one who performs laboratory experiments</note></term> of this art, nor a possessor of the Great Work, and cannot undertake it due to physical frailty and old age: nevertheless, in my opinion, I have progressed so far in <term>theory<note>original: Theoria</note></term> through **God's** grace and extensive reading, that I may truly say for a fact: in this small book is briefly composed exactly that which may be found described at great length in the six volumes of the *Chemical Theater* <note>original: Theatri Chymici; a massive 17th-century compendium of alchemical texts</note>, which is useful and necessary for the investigation of this excellent <term>secret<note>original: Secreti</note></term>. Let the seeker of the art read it often and with reflection, and also consider the following verses:

## The First Chapter.

Hermes, who is called a father of the <term>Philosophers</term> <note>original: "Philosophorum"; in this context, it refers specifically to alchemists and natural philosophers</note>, and lived approximately 2000 years before the birth of Christ, speaks thus: In so long a time of my age, I have not ceased to seek and to research, nor spared any labor, until I attained this art and science solely through the inspiration of the living God, who has deigned to reveal it to me, His servant.

To rational men He has given the capacity to judge a thing and to express their opinion, but has permitted no one the opportunity to sin. However, if I did not fear the Day of Judgment, or the damnation of my soul for concealing this science, I would have revealed nothing of it, nor indicated anything thereof to anyone. But I have wished to show my bounden duty to the faithful in return, just as the Author of Faith deigned to bestow the same upon me.

So learn now, you children of wisdom, not in a physical manner nor thoughtlessly, to understand the science of the ancient sages regarding the four elements, which elements, according to their manner and with their hidden operation, are passive. For their operation is very hidden, because none has its effect unless they are <insert>placed</insert> together

<meta>catchword: placed:</meta>

<summary>This page introduces Hermes Trismegistus as the father of alchemy, who claims his knowledge is a divine revelation he feels morally obligated to share. He begins to instruct "children of wisdom" on the hidden operations of the four elements.</summary>
<keywords>Hermes Trismegistus, Alchemy, Four Elements, Divine Revelation, Philosophy, History of Science</keywords>

--- Page 22 ---
<meta>This page continues the alchemical instructions regarding the composition of the "Water of the Wise" and the proportions of the Great Work.</meta>

( o )

established: And nothing reaches perfection unless it has first completed its colors.

Accordingly, you sons of the wise should know that the ancient philosophers <note>original: "Philosophi"</note> divided their water into four parts: one to two, and three to one, of which the third part is the colors, namely of the moisture which coagulates <note>original: "coaguliret"; the process of turning a liquid into a solid or semi-solid state</note> it: but two-thirds of the water are the weights of the wise.

Take one and a half ounces of the moisture, and of the midday-redness <note>original: "Mittags-Röthe"</note>, that is, of the soul of the gold <note>original: "Seel des Goldes"</note>, take the fourth part, which is half an ounce; and of the yellow sulfur <note>original: "Seyre"; likely a variant of "Schwefel" or "Sulphur"</note> likewise half an ounce, and half of orpiment <term>Auripigment: a deep orange-yellow arsenic sulfide mineral formerly used as a pigment and in alchemy</term>, which makes eight, that is, three ounces.

You should know that the grapevine of the wise is drawn out from three, and its wine is completed at the end with thirty. So understand now the operation: the cooking <note>the process of heating the substance over a long period</note> diminishes it, the tincture increases it, for the moon decreases after fifteen days, and in the third it increases. That is now the beginning and the end.

Behold, I have explained to you what was so hidden, for the work is with you and by you, which you can grasp internally and have remaining in the earth or in the sea. So now preserve the quicksilver <note>original: "Quecksilber"</note>, which is made in the innermost chambers in which it is hardened. For it is the living silver <note>original: "Arg. vivum" or "Argentum vivum"; the Latin term for quicksilver or mercury</note> which takes its name from the earth left behind.

Therefore, whoever hears my words should search further for himself from Him who justifies no evildoer's work, and no benefactor

It emphasizes that the materials for the Great Work are omnipresent and accessible to the seeker.</summary>

->( * ) o ( * )<-

the doer is deprived of the reward. For I have discovered everything of this science <note>original: "Wissenschaft," here referring to the Art of Alchemy</note> that was hidden; the greatest secret and science I have revealed to those who have understanding.

So you shall now know, you seekers of this famous art, and you children of wisdom, that the vulture who stays upon the mountain cries out with a great voice: I am the white of the black, and the red of the white, and the yellow of the red, and certainly I speak the truth. And know, that the beginning of the art is the raven, which flies in the darkness of the night and the clarity of the day, without wings. From the bitterness that is in its throat, the color is taken: from its true body the redness has come forth: and from its back a pure water is taken.

<margin>/</margin>
This you shall now understand, and accept the gift of God, which is hidden from all the unwise. In the caverns of the metals it is hidden, which is the venerable stone, shining in color, an exalted spirit, and an open sea.

<margin>—</margin>
Behold, now I have explained it to you, therefore thank God, who has taught you this science, for He loves the thankful. Accordingly, place it in a moist fire <note>likely a "balneum Mariae" or water bath, providing gentle, consistent heat</note>, and let it cook, which increases the warmth of the moisture, and consumes the drunkenness of the incombustibility <note>referring to the process of purification where volatile elements are stabilized</note>, until the root appears; thereafter draw out from it the redness and the light

<meta>The previous page concluded with instructions on the division of parts; this page continues the chemical proportions and transitions into a moral defense of alchemical secrecy.</meta>

# ) o ( § 13

...light part, until the third part remains behind.

You children of the wise: the philosophers <note>The "Philosophers" here refers to alchemists, the seekers of natural wisdom.</note> are called envious for this reason—not because they begrudged the modest, the God-fearing, the righteous, and the wise. Rather, they withheld knowledge from the ignorant and the wicked, who live without law or good works, so that such people might not become powerful in evil or commit many sins, for which the philosophers would then have to give an account before GOD; for all the wicked are unworthy of wisdom.

You should know that I call this stone by its name, for they <note>The ancient alchemical authorities.</note> have called it the "Wife of Magnesia," or a "Hen," or "White Saliva," "Milk of Flying Creatures," and "Unburnt Ash." They did this so that they might hide it from the unwise—those without understanding, law, or good works—even though I have named it for the intelligent with a familiar name, which is the **Philosopher's Stone**.

Therefore, preserve within it the water, the fire, and the volatile spirit of heaven until the very end. I wish to ask all children of the wise, by our Benefactor who grants us the glory of His grace, that you never reveal the name of this stone to any fool, ignorant person, or unrefined soul. No one has ever given me anything that I have not repaid him for what he gave, and I have not ceased to honor him and to show him kindness.

<summary>The author justifies the traditional secrecy of alchemists as a moral necessity to keep power from the wicked and lists several metaphorical aliases for the Philosopher's Stone.</summary>
<keywords>Philosopher's Stone, alchemical secrecy, Magnesia, ethics of science, volatile spirit</keywords>

--- Page 25 ---
<meta>This page continues the paternal advice and alchemical instruction from the previous section, transitioning from a general introduction to the second chapter of the treatise.</meta>

->) o (<-

My son, this stone is hidden; it is of many colors, and yet born in a single color. Learn to know it and to keep it secret. If the Almighty permits it, you will thereby escape great illnesses, sadness, and everything that can harm and afflict you; through its help, you will come from darkness to light, from the wilderness to a dwelling, and from anxiety and distress to joy and cheerfulness.

---

## CHAPTER II.

My son, above all things I admonish you that you fear God, upon whom all your undertakings rest, as does the union of every person who is set apart from the world. My son, everything that you hear, consider it with reason, for I certainly do not believe that you are deprived of reason or ignorant. Therefore, receive my admonition well and contemplate it, and prepare your heart as if such admonitions came from within yourself.
He who is of a warm nature will not be harmed if he becomes cold: likewise, he who is rational should close the door of ignorance against himself, so that he is not deceived by a false sense of security.
My son, take the volatile <note>original: "Flüchtige" — referring to substances that evaporate or sublimate easily, often symbolized by birds</note>, submerge that which flies, and separate it from its rust <note>original: "Rost" — in alchemy, this refers to the impurities or corrosion on the surface of a metal</note> which corrupts it: draw out the rust and separate it from the substance, so that it may become alive,

<sig>B</sig>

<vocab>Stone, Almighty, Darkness, God, Reason, Nature, Ignorance, Volatile, Rust</vocab>

<summary>The author introduces the "Stone" as a multi-colored yet unified substance capable of healing and spiritual transformation, emphasizing that the work must begin with the fear of God and the application of reason. He provides the first practical alchemical instruction: to stabilize volatile substances and strip away impurities or "rust" to bring the matter to life.</summary>
<keywords>Philosopher's Stone, spiritual healing, alchemy, reason vs. ignorance, purification, volatile substances, rust of metals</keywords>

--- Page 26 ---
<meta>This page continues the alchemical instructions from the previous section, focusing on the stabilization of volatile substances and the purification of the "redness."</meta>

<header>❦ ) 0 ( ❦</header>

...and it be according to your will, when it no longer flies about, but visibly retains the <term>volatile</term> <note>original: "Fliegende"; refers to substances that evaporate or sublimate easily, which the alchemist seeks to "fix" or make solid</note> within itself. For if you will extract it drop by drop, and govern it with understanding during those days known to you, then it will gladly follow you; you shall be its master and subdue it, and be adorned by it.

My son, strip away the shadow and filth from the radiance, because the mists overwhelm it, defile it, and keep it from the light; for it is burned more by its narrowness and by its redness. My son, take this redness which is spoiled by water—just as ashes hide the living fire—which, if you continually strip away from it until you have purified the redness, then it will join together, becoming hard and remaining constant.

My son, take the coals that are deprived of their life and place them back into the water for 30 days, as are known to you; then you shall afterwards be a crowned king, who rests upon the well of that same <term>orpiment</term> <note>original: "Auripigment"; a deep orange-yellow arsenic sulfide mineral used as a pigment and in alchemy to represent the "golden" stage</note> which has no moisture.

Now I have made joyful the hearts of those who hear it, who hope in you, and your eyes which behold you, because of the hope of the Eternal which you encompass within yourself.

My son, it should be known to you that the water was previously in the air, and afterwards in the earth; bring the same back again to the upper regions, and do not reverse it through its passages unskillfully; thereafter, return it to its previously gathered spirit, and add it to it quite sparingly or gradually.

<meta>catchword: You</meta>

<summary>This page provides allegorical instructions for the purification of alchemical substances, specifically focusing on "fixing" volatile elements and cleansing the "redness" to achieve a stable, "crowned" state. It emphasizes the cyclical movement of elements between air and earth and the importance of patient, gradual processing.</summary>
<keywords>volatility, purification, orpiment, alchemy, fixation, spiritual transformation, elements</keywords>

--- Page 27 ---
<meta>Continuity established with the previous page's discussion on the composition of the Stone. The catchword "The" (Der) from the previous page leads into the description of the ointments.</meta>

# )( 0 )(

You should know, my son, that the ointment of our earth is the <term>Sulphur</term> <note>Sulfur, representing the active, masculine principle in alchemy</note>, <term>Auripigment</term> <note>Orpiment, an arsenic sulfide mineral used for its golden color</note>, <term>Sireth</term> <note>An archaic alchemical term, likely referring to a specific mineral salt or resin</note>, and <term>Colcothar</term> <note>Vitriol or iron oxide remaining after distillation</note>, which is a sulfur, orpiment, and the like, each of which is lesser than the other, and in which there is a distinction. From these also comes the <term>Unguentum collae</term> <note>Latin: "Glue-ointment"; likely referring to a sticky, binding substance</note>, which consists of hair, nails, and sulfur; from this also comes <term>Rock Oil</term> <note>Petroleum or naphtha</note>, and the "brain," which is the orpiment. From this also comes the "Cat’s Claw," which is *Sireth*, from which also comes the Ointment of the Sages, and the ointment of two Oriental Mercuries, which pursue the sulfur and seize the bodies.

I say further, that the sulfur tints and hardens, and is retained, and this occurs through the union of the tinctures. However, those same ointments tint and then fly away again, being contained within the body, which is an ounce of the <term>Aluminous Sulfur</term> <note>Sulfur derived from or associated with alum</note> of the volatile composition, and which retains the volatile substances.

My son, the <term>Philosophers</term> <note>The ancient alchemists</note> have discovered and established an order in our Egg; however, this is by no means found in a common hen’s egg. But so that such great divine wisdom might not be destroyed, the composition is taken from the four elements and thus brought together.

You should know, my son, that a hen’s egg is a great remedy and kin to nature, for in it is an airy power and a proportion of the elements, and the earth is, by nature, gold.

You should know, my son, that the mediocre <term>Unguent</term> <note>An ointment or oily substance; in alchemy, this often refers to the "fat of the earth" or a metallic oil.</note>, which is the Fire, is a medium between the vessels and the water, seeing as they are called Ointments and <term>Sulphurs</term> <note>Sulphur represents the active, combustible, and masculine principle in alchemy.</note>; for between Fire, Oil, and Sulphur there is a very close relationship, since just as the fire ignites, so also does the sulphur.

In these wondrous, hidden elements are the arts of all occurrences, which it has brought to completion and attained. Therefore, he who wishes to be initiated into this, our secret wisdom, must drive away from himself the vice of vainglory: He must be God-fearing, pious, and profound, friendly toward people, of cheerful gestures, greeting others gladly, and must well preserve the secrets revealed to him.

But if he should know such things, he will be in great dignity, so that even kings will honor him. These secrets, my son, we must well preserve, and keep them hidden from all the godless and the unwise.

The Stone is composed and put together from many things and various colors, as well as from the four elements <note>Earth, Air, Fire, and Water</note> united; these we must separate and divide into members, and set those members apart. In part, one must kill the property that is within it: preserving the water and fire that dwell therein, which is retained in its own water from the four elements and their waters. This is namely not a water according to its form, but a fire, which rises above the water and holds the spirits <note>volatile substances</note> together in a clean and good vessel, so that they do not depart from the bodies; for in this way they become constant and are able to tincture <note>term: tingiren; the alchemical process of dyeing or transforming a base metal into a noble one</note>.

O you blessed, acidic water-form, you who dissolve the elements! We must, however, mix this watery soul with our vinegar, so that we may retain the Sulphuric form. For when the power of the water and the composite is dissolved, it is the key to renewal. Then death and blackness <note>term: nigredo; the initial stage of the alchemical work characterized by putrefaction</note> depart from them, and the work of wisdom gains its progress.

## CHAPTER V.

My son, what is born of the <term>Raven</term> <note>A symbol for the "Nigredo" or black stage of alchemy, representing the initial putrefaction and death of the matter</note> is the beginning of this Art. Behold, with great verbosity I have darkened the word for you and deprived it of light; and that which is dissolved I have called the composite, and the nearest I have called the furthest or most remote. Therefore, roast the things, then cook them with that which comes from the body of the <term>Horse</term> <note>Likely a reference to "horse dung," used in alchemy to provide a gentle, steady heat for fermentation</note> for seven, or 14, or 21 days.

Then it will become a <term>Dragon</term> <note>A symbol for the volatile and fixed principles united, or the acidic solvent</note> that consumes its own wings and kills itself. When that is accomplished, lay it in a cloth rag and into the fire in the oven, and <note>Original text has "uud" for "und"</note> guard it diligently so that it does not escape from the vessel.

And this you should know, that the time of the earth is in the water, and it lasts so long until you lead it over it again. <note>This likely refers to the process of "solution," where the solid material is dissolved into a liquid state.</note>

After it has now become soft and burnt, take its brain <note>A metaphorical term for the essential core or "mercury" of the substance</note>, and rub it in the sharpest vinegar, or child's urine, until it becomes dark. When that has happened, it lives in the putrefaction <note>original: "Fäulung"; a stage in alchemy where the matter decomposes and turns black</note>; the dark clouds that were in it before it died in its body, those will be transformed. But when this is repeated as I have described it, it dies once more and, as I have said, it lives thereafter.

In its life, however, as also in its death, we use the <term>Spirits</term> <note>original: "Spiritus"; volatile substances or vapors that can be distilled and recombined</note>. For just as it dies when the spirits are taken away, so it becomes alive again when the <term>Spirits</term> are given back to it, and it rejoices over them. But when you have come so far, you will find in truth that which you seek.

I must also tell you the signs of joy, and what makes its body <term>fixed</term> <note>original: "fix"; the state where a substance can withstand the heat of fire without evaporating</note>.

Through these figurative speeches your ancestors, after they reached their purpose—behold, there they died. Now I have shown you the goal, and opened a book for the experienced; hidden things I have concealed, that which was set apart I have made so that it may be kept, different forms I have joined together, and the <term>Spirits</term> I have associated with them. Accept this gift from God.

### CHAPTER VI.

You should rightly thank dear God, who bestows upon every wise man the Art <note>original: "Kunst"; refers to Alchemy, the "Great Art"</note> which frees us from misery and poverty. With all its power and essence, and with demonstrable miracles, I will attempt it, and humbly pray to the highest God that we may yet come to Him during our lifetime.

Therefore, you children of the wisdom, the ointments <note>original: "Unguenta"; medicinal or alchemical salves used as a base for transformations</note> which we extract from nails, hair, verdigris <note>original: "Spangrün"; a green pigment or rust of copper</note>, tragacanth <note>original: "Traganth"; a natural gum used as a binder</note>, and bones, are written of in the books of the ancients.

<margin>☞</margin>
Furthermore, we must explain how one should prepare the ointment which contains the tincture <note>original: "Tinctur"; the concentrated essence capable of transmuting metals</note> within itself, which makes volatile substances hard and cleanses the sulfur <note>original: "Sulphur"; in alchemy, sulfur represents the active, combustible principle of a substance</note>, and how it should be preferred above all other perfect ointments. For we know and recognize the preciousness of its vessel and essence, which is called the "Divine Sulfur." It has a form like other ointments; it is the hidden and buried ointment which is of humble appearance and dwells in its body like fire dwells in trees and stones, which we must extract with very subtle art and understanding without burning it.

We must, however, separate two sulfurs <note>original: "Sulphura"</note> from others. You should know, my son, that he who does not know the difference knows much less of the two sulfurs <note>original: "Schweffel"</note>: Not that there are exactly two sulfurs which are sublimated from the ointment of the stones, the tincture

When that has happened, we possess the greatest wisdom and a secret of all secrets.

## CHAPTER VII.

You dear sons of the Wise, there are seven bodies, the first of which is their most perfect Gold, a king and a head, which neither the earth can corrupt, nor burning things destroy, nor water change; for its <term>Complexion</term> <note>Complexion: the specific balance of the four humors or qualities (hot, cold, moist, dry) within a substance</note> is balanced, and

<summary>This page describes the alchemical process of uniting 'Sulfurs' with a body through a watery medium and introduces Gold as the first and most perfect of the seven metallic bodies.</summary>
<keywords>Sulfur, Tincture, Water, Gold, Dissolution, Seven Bodies, Alchemy, Complexion</keywords>

--- Page 39 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the perfection of metals, specifically focusing on the role of gold and the necessity of fermentation in the alchemical process.</meta>

# 31

Its nature is balanced and correct in heat, cold, and moisture, and there is nothing superfluous within it. For this reason, the Sages have preferred it over other bodies and held it in very high regard, saying that gold possesses such a quality among the bodies as the sun is brighter with its light among the stars. For through the sun, by **God's** command, all plants and all fruits of the earth reach their perfection. Thus, gold contains every body within itself, making them alive; and the <term>Ixlir</term> <note>original: "Ixlir," a variant of Elixir, the transformative substance sought by alchemists</note> is the ferment, without which it cannot be prepared at all.

For just as a lump of <term>Theich</term> <note>original: "Theich," an archaic term for dough</note> cannot be leavened without sourdough, so it is when you have sublimated and purified the <term>Corpus</term> <note>the "body" or physical substance</note> and have separated the filth from the <term>Fecibus</term> <note>Latin: "faeces," meaning dregs, sediment, or waste matter</note>. When you then wish to compose it and mix it together, put the <term>Ferment</term> <note>the agent that induces transformation or "ripening"</note> therein, and turn the water into earth until the <term>Ixlir</term> is fermented and the <term>Massa</term> <note>the bulk or "mass" of the material</note> becomes a ferment itself.

Consider and observe whether the ferment of the thing is taken from anywhere else but its own nature. See now how no <term>Ferment</term> can exist except from that same <term>Massa</term>. And note that the ferment makes the <term>Confection</term> <note>the prepared mixture</note> white, prevents burning, and holds the <term>Tinctur</term> <note>the spiritual essence or coloring power</note> to itself so that it does not flee, and gladdens the bodies, making it possible for them to be joined together and penetrate one another.

And this is the key of the Sages and the end of the work; through this science, the bodies are improved, and their work is brought to completion through the will of **God**.

<meta>catchword: Through</meta>

<summary>This page explains the alchemical importance of gold as a perfect, solar body and details the necessity of a 'ferment' (elixir) to transform and stabilize the matter. # ❦ ) 0 ( ❦

Through carelessness, however, and by holding a poor opinion of it, the work is corrupted, just as the ferment <note>yeast or leaven</note> in the dough, and the rennet and milk for the cheese, and the musk among the spices.

There is a certain gold-color tending toward redness, and its property is not a sweetness; therefore, we have prepared from it the silk-work, which is the Elixir <note>original: "Ixlir," a common archaic spelling of Elixir</note>: and from it we have established the glass-painting work <gloss>the glazes</gloss> that we have written about, and with the King's seal we dye the clay, and therein we have placed the color of the heaven, which strengthens the sight of those who gaze upon it.

Therefore, gold is the most precious stone of all, which has no blemishes and is tempered, which neither fire, nor air, nor water, nor earth can destroy; a universal ferment, which brings everything to its proper state through moderate composition, and which is of a pale or lemon color.

The Gold of the Sages <note>The "Philosophers' Gold," a perfected state of matter rather than common bullion</note>, when well-cooked and made properly ripe through the fiery water, brings about the Elixir. For the Gold of the Sages is heavier than lead, which, through moderate composition, is the ferment of the Elixir. And on the contrary, it becomes misshapen because of immoderate composition.

For the first work is made from a growing thing <note>vegetable kingdom</note>, the other from an animal in a hen <note>likely referring to the use of eggs or albumin in alchemical processes</note>; this is a great aid and constancy of the elements, and our earth is gold, from which we establish all silk-work, which is

# THE OPENER
## OF RAYMOND LULL
### CONCERNING
# THE PHILOSOPHER’S STONE.

The Sages witness that there is but one Stone, and that the same alone is composed from the four elements, and requires no external thing, unless it be of its own nature.

First of all, it is purified of all superfluity and phlegmatic, destructive matter, which is its death, and which kills the spirit that has the power to make its earth living. Therefore, it shall be purified, and the destructive phlegm separated from it through a subtle art, which I shall tell you.

For if it is not well purified, then its earth will also not be purified nor made white: likewise, no marriage between the body and spirit will be able to occur.

And thus is that same spirit then called the spirit of the <term>Stone <note>original: "Lapidis"</note></term>, which can make its dead <term>Body <note>original: "Corpus"</note></term> living, from which <term>Composed body <note>original: "Composito corpore"</note></term> it has gone forth through its <term>Preparation <note>original: "Præparation"</note></term>.

<summary>This opening page of Raymond Lull's treatise establishes the fundamental alchemical principle that the Philosopher's Stone is a single substance derived from the four elements, requiring rigorous purification to unite body and spirit.</summary>
<keywords>Raymond Lull, Philosopher's Stone, four elements, purification, phlegm, alchemical marriage, body and spirit, preparation</keywords>

--- Page 44 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the alchemical resurrection process, following the transition from the "death" of the matter to its glorified state.</meta>

# 36 ☿ ) 0 ( ♃

And when, accordingly, the **Body** <note>original: "Corpus"</note> has come again from death to life, it is clear, pure, white, and shining, possessing great **fineness** <note>original: "Subtilität"; refers to the physical refinement and permeability of the matter</note>, and clothed in the most beautiful garments.

You should know that when this same **fiery spirit** enters into its body and causes it to rise again, the **Philosophers** <note>The "Philosophers" refers to the historical lineage of alchemists</note> call it the **Blessed Stone**, as well as the **Stone of Great Worth and Honor**, namely after its resurrection. And this is understood to be the fiery and most noble spirit of the aforementioned **Stone** <note>original: "Lapidis"</note>, which is thus illuminated within the nature of the four elements.

However, it is not to be understood as being the Earth alone, or the Water alone, nor the Air, nor the Fire alone; rather, it is only one single essence, which contains within itself the nature and property of the four elements simultaneously. For otherwise, it could not be joined with its Earth, and subsequently, the Earth could not be made white by that same spirit if it did not have something of its nature within itself.

Therefore, care must always be taken that the nature and property which resides in that same spirit is not burnt away during its **preparation** <note>original: "Præparation"</note> by the power of the fire. For in that case, the spirit would never have the power within itself to whiten, or to **unite** <note>original: "conjungiren"</note> with the Earth.

For the **Medium** <note>The "Mittel" or intermediary substance</note> and the **Soul**, which has communion with both death and life, would [be separated] from the spirit by the power of the fire—

It warns that excessive heat during preparation can destroy the soul-link that connects the spirit to the physical matter.</summary>

--- Page 45 ---
<meta>This page continues the alchemical instructions regarding the separation and preservation of the vital spirits during distillation, emphasizing the danger of excessive heat.</meta>

<page-num>37</page-num>
<header>✽ ) o ( ✽</header>

...form, so that the Spirit would remain dead without the Soul; thus the Soul would be removed from it and fly into the region of its own sphere. <note>The author uses 'Spirit' and 'Soul' as distinct alchemical principles that must remain joined to animate the 'Body' or physical matter.</note>

And this mystery is not to be revealed to the foolish and ignorant. You should know that those who believe they are making a Water of Life <note>original: "Wasser des Lebens" or *Aqua Vitae*</note> are certainly making a Water of Death, because of the combustion.

Therefore, I diligently pray that you take heed of the fire during the preparation, so that it is not destroyed by too great a heat. For if the Soul were to depart, the Spirit would not have the power to make its Body alive, because it could not give that which it did not have within itself.

And note that the preparation of this Spirit proceeds thus: Take two or three pounds of the best juice of the <term>Lunaria</term> <note>Moonwort; a plant often associated with silver and lunar alchemy</note> that you can find, and of the strongest quality; place it in a vessel with a <term>Helm</term> <note>The 'head' or cap of a still (alembic) where vapors condense</note>, ensuring the joints are well closed and <term>luted</term> <note>Sealed with an airtight paste or clay</note>.

Place it in a small furnace, make a fire beneath it from a single candle, and <term>distill</term> <note>The process of heating a liquid to create vapor and then cooling it back into liquid to purify it</note> the aforementioned spirits with a subtle and gentle fire; and distill until it begins to form "veins" <note>Refers to the visible streaks or 'legs' of liquid running down the glass during distillation</note>.

Then you can thereby separate the thickness of the airy souls. Accordingly, when the <term>Phlegma</term> <note>The watery, tasteless, or impure byproduct of distillation</note> begins to come or form veins, it is a sign that the <term>Spirit</term> <note>The volatile, active essence of the substance</note> has been distilled, which contains all the perfection of life within itself.

->) 0 (<-

And then take that same distilled *Spirit* <note>original: "Spiritum"; in alchemy, this refers to the volatile, active essence of a substance</note>, and preserve it very well in a glass vessel sealed tightly with white wax.

Afterward, attach another receiver <note>original: "Vorlage"; the vessel used to collect the condensed liquid</note> to the helm <note>original: "Helm"; the top part of the still, also known as an alembic</note>, and collect the second water, for this still contains some of the sublimated *Spirit* within it, although it is not as strong as the first.

Distill this second water until nothing rises but the *phlegm* <note>original: "phlegma"; the watery, inactive, and tasteless component of a substance</note>, which has no other quality than that of spoiled water when one tastes a little of it with the mouth in the common way.

If it still possesses some strength, then you may distill it further; but if it tastes like any other pure water, then remove the receiver with the second water and put another clean receiver in its place to receive the entire *phlegm* therein, and distill until nothing more comes over.

And then pour out the entire *phlegm*, for it is this which brings death to our precious stone <note>The "Philosopher's Stone," the ultimate goal of the alchemical work</note>. And this the common man does not recognize, but we know it.

Now you have the earth, which has remained at the bottom of the vessel, entirely black in the form of melted pitch.

For the *calcination* <note>original: "Calcinatio"; the process of reducing a solid to a powder or ash through heat</note> of the earths cannot happen through a strong fire, as the *sophists* <note>A derogatory term used by true alchemists for those who practice the art falsely or without understanding</note> believe; rather, it happens through its own *Spirit*, which preserves it from burning.

For its spirit draws its soul from its *body* <note>original: "Cörper"; the physical matter remaining after the volatile parts have been removed</note>, and drives its remaining *phlegm* away.

and brings the Earth to death, and afterwards makes it eternally alive again.

And take great care that the <term>Spirit</term><note>The volatile, active essence of the substance.</note> does not escape from the fuming dregs, for therein is the <term>Sulphur</term><note>In alchemy, Sulphur represents the principle of combustibility and soul.</note>, which has the power to <term>congeal</term><note>To solidify or freeze into a stable form.</note> our water into a crystalline plate, or into the form of a most white powder, which is like the most beautiful snow.

Afterward, place a <term>still-head</term><note>original: "Helm"; the top part of a distilling apparatus.</note> upon it, <term>lute</term><note>To seal the joints of the apparatus with clay or paste to prevent leaks.</note> it well, and distill its <term>Spirit</term> through the fire with a light in a subtle manner, as I have said before, until you see that no more veins—which the <term>Spirits</term> create—are being formed and destroyed again at the top of the <term>still-head</term>.

And then, remove the <term>receiving vessel</term><note>original: "Vorlage"; the container that catches the distillate.</note> of the <term>still-head</term> containing the <term>Spirits</term> and keep them separately; then put another <term>receiving vessel</term> in its place and distill further to see if more <term>Spirit</term> is present therein. If not, the water that is distilled will have a taste like common warm spring water.

Pour that out once again, for such <term>phlegmatic water</term><note>The watery, inactive, and "dead" portion of a liquid.</note> also brings death to our <term>Stone</term><note>The Philosopher's Stone, the ultimate goal of the work.</note>.

And when the distillation <note>original: "Destilarion"</note> has occurred, then remove the vessel with all the material, which you will then find to be harder than it was before. The reason is this: because the spirit has then drawn into itself the airy soul from its body, for it is of the nature in which the soul is retained.

Repeat this work as often as necessary until you see that the material <note>original: "Materia"</note> at the bottom is calcined <term>calcined: reduced to a powder through repeated heating</term> into a black powder: or also until you see that it yields no more phlegm <term>phlegm: a watery, tasteless byproduct of distillation</term> nor taste, as it did at first.

And it should not be unknown to you that in the distillation you must mix the two spirits <note>original: "Spiritus"</note> with the earth, until the earth and the spirit show the aforementioned signs—namely, that the earth is calcined and the spirit yields no phlegm.

You should know, dearest son, that in this first chapter a great secret is contained, regarding the extraction of the soul from the body, through which its spirit is made alive, just as the body is killed by the soul's absence.

Therefore, draw out the moisture which kills the natural heat, for that same heat is a cause of the birth of our sulfur <note>original: "Sulphuris"</note>.

Thus the philosophers <note>original: "Philosophi"</note> testify that our sulfur cannot be born without heat. For we then draw this heat from death into life, because it was previously dead in the black sulfur; but now it is alive, and clothed with great virtues, power, and strength.

And you should know that this same warmth must now be able to penetrate all parts of its spirit, because of its sharpness: It should also now be able to *coagulate* <note>original: "coaguliren"; to thicken or solidify</note>, because of its dryness.

For it is written that every dry thing gladly drinks in its moisture, and transforms it into *Sulfur* <note>original: "Sulphur"; in alchemy, this represents the active, combustible, and masculine principle</note>, which afterwards has the power to *coagulate* the entire *quicksilver* <note>original: "argentum vivum"; mercury, representing the fluid, passive, and feminine principle</note>.

Look closely, my dear son, when this inversion or transformation is observed, there is found no *contrariety* <note>opposition</note> of sense in the birthing. For every birth must happen through the distinction of its *corruption* and *generation*, which are simultaneously opposing things: And the destruction, which is powerful within the *matter* <note>original: "Materia"</note>, leads it so that it can come to the new birth of eternal life.

And for that reason, the destruction necessarily had to happen through the separation of the elements, after whose *preparation* <note>original: "Præparation"</note> their *Sulfur* is created.

And from that same substance, you will afterwards be able to make the *medicine* of the hidden stone, into which such power of the *Sulfur* has flowed, which can make things living, and bind two *opposites* <note>original: "Contraria"</note> together—namely the *body* <note>original: "Corpus"</note> and the *spirit* <note>original: "Spiritum"</note>—in which it makes the *body* subtle, and mixes it with the *spirit*, and *congeals* <note>original: "congeliret"</note> and retains the *body* within the *spirit*, as well as the spirit within the body, with an everlasting *fixation* <note>the process of making a volatile substance stable under heat</note>.

And of this the *Philosopher* <note>likely referring to Hermes Trismegistus or Aristotle, often cited as "The Philosopher" in alchemical texts</note> testifies when he speaks: We can make no transition from

from the softness of **Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurii*</note> to the hardness of the metal, without a middle-disposition, which has community with both the one and the other; and through such community, it has the power to bind in an immediate way and to create unity.

If you are now a seeker of the truth, then understand that three have become one. And of this the **Philosopher** <note>Likely referring to Hermes Trismegistus or Aristotle, often cited as "The Philosopher" in alchemical texts.</note> testifies in the *Book of the Trinity* <note>original: *Buch der Dreyheit*</note>, where he speaks: Our precious stone is **composed** of three, namely of spirit, body, and soul.

You should also know, alongside this, that the spirit is sometimes the medium between the body and the soul: For the spirit draws the soul from the body and kills the **body** <note>original: *Corpus*</note>, and afterwards it gives the soul back to it and awakens the same.

Of such a resurrection, Hermes speaks to all seekers of the art and truth thus: O Son, take the soul of the stone and set it into our white foliated earth, which is volatile, unless you wish for it to lose its coarse elements. And know that the soul holds it in such a way that it cannot fly when it ascends into heaven and descends to the earth; for the **Spirit** <note>original: *Spiritus*</note> takes the power of the body unto itself in such a manner that there is nothing that can prevail against it. Thus, death will no longer be able to kill it; because it stands written that once it is awakened into life, it shall never die again.

cannot be, where it has not previously been dead, for death is a cause of the glorification <note>original: "Herrlichmachung"</note> of the <term>body</term> <note>original: "Cörpers"; refers here to the physical matter in the alchemical vessel</note> in eternal life: just as decay is also a cause of the new birth of a divine substance.

And in such a manner we say that the spirit is the life of the soul when it encompasses the soul within itself: so we also call the spirit a <term>body</term>, because of its tangible and visible physicality, of which it is composed, and is also placed in a location. And this is the reason why this <term>spirit</term> <note>original: "Spiritus"</note> contains within itself the nature of the <term>body</term>.

And beyond these two properties just mentioned, you should know this third thing between the body and the soul, which we call the "Middle," that can unite and bind them together without separation. And afterwards the <term>spirit</term> encompasses the soul within itself, through which the dead <term>body</term> is made alive again.

So now this is the moving cause, that the <term>conjunction</term> <note>original: "Conjunctio"; the alchemical marriage or union of opposites</note> of the body and the spirit happens through natural love and friendly disposition.

For such a friendly **disposition** <note>original: "Disposition" (arrangement)</note> and **conjunction** <note>original: "Conjunctio" (joining)</note> of love is that through which the union of the spirit and the body is brought about, so that they become one **substance** of an inseparable **body**. Within this exists the density of the body and its power, along with all those figures which primarily enter into the essence from the subtle spirit.

Its density is also far removed into the **substance** with the subtle spirit, in such a subtle manner that there is no physical eye with which anything else can be seen except a dissolution of the spirits, in the form of water: And thus, in an invisible way, the soul and the density of the body rest there together.

And then it is also to be seen how the soul is bound by the power and property of the body, and the soul is then also of the nature and density of that same **body**.

Therefore, when the **body** <note>original: "Corpus"</note> is dead from thirst, it then has a longing to moisten its thirst and to drink, so that its moist nature may be restored to it—that nature which it lost in such an exceeding and powerful way during its **calcination** <term>Calcination: the process of heating a substance to high temperatures to drive off volatile matter or effect oxidation; here used metaphorically for a spiritual or chemical "drying out."</term>. And so, through life-giving restoration, it draws back to itself the nature which it had lost, and enters into its own nature: Indeed, it even draws that which is increased from its own **substance**, made abundant through the property of its **body**, which is poured out through the entire **substance** of the spirit.

And thus is such <term>Extraction<note>original: *Extractio*; the process of drawing out the essential spirit or essence from a raw material</note></term>, which has occurred through the aforementioned friendliness and love of Nature, a cause of the <term>Conjunction<note>original: *Conjunctio*; the alchemical marriage or joining of two opposing substances, usually "body" and "spirit"</note></term> or joining together of the body and the spirit.

And of such Conjunction, the <term>Philosopher<note>original: *Philosophus*; likely referring to Hermes Trismegistus or another foundational alchemical authority</note></term> speaks to the Son of Truth thus: Where the Conjunction of the body and spirit shall not have occurred, there shall nevermore a conception take place: And where there will be no conception, there can nevermore a pregnancy occur: And where there is no pregnancy, there can never be a birth of a child: And thus, in this manner, the power of such Conjunction can be realized.

And for this reason, the Philosopher reveals that the birth of our <term>Stone<note>original: *Stein* or *Lapis*; the Philosopher's Stone, the ultimate goal of the alchemical work</note></term> is compared to human birth, and indeed for this reason: For when a child is born, one must nourish it for so long until it is quite strong and can well endure the fire or the <term>substance of the fire<note>original: *Feuers Substanz*</note></term>.

And you should not think that you have finished at the point where you should only just begin, for you have not yet seen the secret? which is reserved to be shown to the children of truth, and that very thing now begins here in the second part, concerning the <term>Nourishing of the Stone<note>original: *Ernährung Des Lapidis*</note></term>.

The more that the great transformations of the Stone are revealed here, as much as is possible; so much more will you see the transformation, and the secrets of many wondrous things, and the power of Nature will be visibly revealed before your eyes.

And such a <term>Secretum<note>Latin: "Secret." ## The Vivification of the Earth.

The physical, solid remains of a substance after distillation or calcination.</note></term> through a natural inversion.

But this same thing cannot happen through force, or through a natural wisdom; rather, the <term>Ingenium<note>Latin: "Innate character" or "natural engine."</note></term> of nature is the location of our <term>Sulphur<note>In alchemy, Sulphur represents the active, masculine, and combustible principle of a substance.</note></term>, namely in the <term>complexion<note>The specific balance of qualities (hot, cold, moist, dry) within a substance.</note></term> and in the fact that they allow themselves to be united.

And from this one has the necessary cause, namely that the seminal grain, which is moist and warm, can transform this.

# The Second Part,
### Concerning the Completion of the Medicine.

Now, furthermore, through the second part, the Stone <note>original: "Lapis"</note> is colored, fixed, and fermented. The ferment of the Stone for the White Work <note>The stage of Albedo, or whitening, which produces silver.</note> is silver, and for the Red Work <note>The stage of Rubedo, or reddening, which produces gold.</note> is gold, as the philosophers prove. For without ferment, neither the Sun <note>original: "Sol"; alchemical shorthand for Gold.</note> nor the Moon <note>original: "Luna"; alchemical shorthand for Silver.</note> comes forth, nor anything else that is of its nature.

Therefore, join the ferment with its sulfur, and it will be able to give birth to its own kind. For the ferment draws the sulfur to its color, as well as to its nature, and to its weight, and likewise to its taste <note>The catchword "schmack" from the bottom of the original page indicates the next word is "Geschmack" (taste).</note>

## More regarding the mineral stone.

Take a fourth part of the congealed stone and mix it into the previously mentioned *amalgam* <note>A mixture of mercury with another metal, in this case likely silver or gold</note>, and rub them together until they become a single *body*. Repeat this work whenever you wish to *multiply*, for the more you repeat this work, the more your *medicine* will be increased in power and strength, namely thus: If one part of the *medicine* transforms 100 parts the first time, it will transform 1,000 the second time. The third time it will transform 10,000, and so on infinitely. Therefore, do not neglect this work of *multiplication*, for the *medicine* is thus better united, more *digested* <note>Refined or matured through steady heat</note> in its parts, and is more perfect in its effect.

For no element can exist without the division of its nature, because no single element contains all parts within itself; and through this one element alone, the Stone can neither be corrupted nor generated within itself, for it is not the nature of the Spirit. And Son, understand this: that our Stone comes from nothing else but a pure nature alone, which every wise man will know through two paths: first, through contemplative wisdom—that is, through the moderation of the intellect—and through experience, with perfect enlightenment.

Although we have written very many books concerning various works of our philosophical art <note>The "philosophical art" refers to Alchemy, the practice of transforming matter and seeking the Philosopher's Stone.</note>; nevertheless, we wish to prefer this, our latest little book, over the others. It is rightly titled by us *Elucidatio Testamenti*, or an Elucidation <note>original: "Elucidatio Testamenti", meaning an explanation or shedding of light upon a testament or will.</note>. For whatever we concealed with great long-windedness in the philosophical writings of our *Testament* and *Codicil* <note>original: "Testaments und Codicilli"; these refer to the author's earlier, more famous alchemical works.</note>, we show that very same thing quite clearly in this our little book, and explain it in the briefest manner, so that it will no longer be necessary to write any more books. For because the *Composition* <note>original: "Compositio", the process of putting together or synthesizing.</note> or preparation of our books is nothing other than an artistic dexterity of a subtle *Genius* <note>original: "Ingenij", referring to the natural wit or intellect of the author.</note> to hide our art—which is shown at great length in our books—the same shall now be brought here out of the darkness into a bright light, which before me no *Philosopher* <note>original: "Philosophus"; in this context, a practitioner of alchemy or "natural philosophy".</note> has ever dared to do. And we compose this little book in six chapters, in which the entire secret of this art is revealed with very clear words. Among the six chapters:

The first is of the *Matter* <note>original: "Materia", the raw substance used to begin the alchemical process.</note> of our Stone.
The second is of the Vessel.

### CHAPTER I.

## Concerning the *Materia* <note>The physical substance or raw material.</note> of our *Stone*.

Now we wish first to begin by giving instruction concerning the *Materia* of our Stone. For we have introduced foreign things into our *Magistery* <note>The "Masterwork" or the complete alchemical process.</note> for the sake of allegory, even though there is only one single thing belonging to our Stone, to which we add nothing, nor take anything away. We have also described three *Stones*, namely a Mineral, Animal, and Vegetable one, yet there is only one single Stone in our Art.

O you dear sons (of Wisdom), we wish to give you to understand that the Stone is composed of three: namely of Body, Soul, and Spirit. It is called Mineral because it is a *Minera* <note>original: "Minera" — a mineral ore or "earth-juice," a thing dug out of the earth</note>; it is called Animal because it has a soul; and it is called Vegetable because it grows and is multiplied <note>increased</note> like the things that grow from the earth. In this lies hidden all the secrets of our masterpiece, which
<margin>+</margin>
is Gold, Silver, and the *Aqua vitæ* <note>original: "Aqua vitæ" — literally "Water of Life," referring here to a potent mercurial solvent or the "spirit" of the substance.</note>. The *Aqua vitæ*, however, is the soul and the life

For it is almost imperishable and incorruptible, like the heavens in the incessant circulation of their movement.

### CHAPTER III.
## Concerning the Furnace.

We wish now to say something of our furnace: It will, however, be quite difficult to recount the secret of our furnace, which the ancient philosophers <note>original: "Philosophi"</note> kept so very hidden.

We have indeed mentioned many kinds of furnaces in our books, yet we use only one single furnace, which is called *Athanor* <term>Athanor: a self-feeding furnace designed to maintain a constant heat over long periods</term>, which is translated as an "immortal fire," for it provides a fire that lasts constantly at the same degree from the beginning to the end of our *Stone* <note>original: "Lapidis," referring to the Philosopher's Stone</note>, and which makes the same living and mature.

My son, pay attention to our words, so you will understand that our furnace is composed of two pieces or parts, and that the joints thereof fit together correctly and are well sealed. Its nature is as follows: Make a furnace large or small, according to whether there is much or little matter <note>original: "Materi"</note>. For the quantity of the matter requires a large furnace; but if there is little of it, one needs only a small furnace, in the manner of a

### CHAPTER IV.
## Concerning the Fire.

Although we have taught in our books of a threefold <term>fire</term>, namely a natural one, one not natural, and one against nature, as well as other different modes of our fire; nevertheless, we wish here to make understood only a single <term>fire</term>, which is composed of many things. It is the greatest mystery to come to the knowledge of it, because it is not a human but an angelic task to reveal this heavenly gift.

However, so that the curse which the <term>philosophers</term> have left to posterity does not fall upon us, we pray to GOD that this treasure of our secret fire may reach no one—

My son, incline your ears to hear that our fire is composed of two things. For the Creator of all things has created two things which, among others, are suitable for this fire: namely, horse manure and unslaked lime. Their mixture makes our fire, the artistic invention of which is conducted as follows.

Take *Ventrem equi* <note>Latin: "belly of the horse," a common alchemical term for horse manure used as a gentle, steady heat source</note>, that is, horse manure that is well digested, one part, and one part of pure living lime <note>unslaked lime or calcium oxide</note>. These mixed together and placed tightly upon one another in our furnace, with the vessel containing the *Materia Lapidis* <note>Latin: "matter of the stone"</note> placed in the center, and the furnace diligently sealed, you will then have an excellent fire without light and without coals. When it is thus placed in its furnace, it cannot be or act otherwise than as necessity requires.

### CHAPTER V.
## Concerning the Decoction.

There are also many kinds of preparation for our Stone recounted in our Testament and our other books: namely, *Solutio* <note>dissolving</note>, *coagulatio* <note>thickening</note>, *sublimatio* <note>vaporizing and solidifying</note>, *distillatio* <note>purifying by steam</note>, *calcinatio* <note>reducing to ash by heat</note>, *separatio* the parting, *fusio* <note>melting</note>, *inceratio* <note>softening to a waxy state</note>, *imbibitio* <note>soaking</note>, *fixio* <note>making a substance resistant to fire</note>, etc. The meaning of all these is comprised under the name of Decoction <note>the process of boiling or heating to extract essence</note>.

The art of our <term>Decoction</term>, however, is this: that you place the <term>Matter of the Compound</term> <note>original: "Compositi Materiam"</note> according to its weight into its vessel, furnace, and fire, and boil it constantly until it reaches its full maturity. This can be completed in ten months without any manual labor, from the beginning to the end of the entire <term>Masterpiece</term>.

We have, however, intended to convey the excellence of our Art through the aforementioned manifold <term>Knots</term> <note>original: "Nodos"; refers to the complex difficulties or riddles of the process</note>, so that the understanding of the unwise might be surrounded and covered by a foggy curtain, and the unwise might not reach the summit of our secret mountain; but rather persist and remain in error until the Sun and Moon become one globe—which is impossible to happen without the will and command of GOD.

### CHAPTER VI.

## Concerning the <term>Tincture</term> and <term>Multiplication</term> or Increase of our <term>Stone</term>.

Finally, we wish to speak of the <term>Tincture</term> <note>The medicinal or transformative essence of the Stone</note> and <term>Multiplication</term>, which is the end and the conclusion of the entire <term>Mastery</term> <note>original: "Magisterii"</note>.

We have indeed indicated various <term>Methods of Projection</term> <note>original: "Modi projectionis"; the act of casting the Stone onto base metals to transform them</note> of our <term>Tincture</term> in our books, yet there is no difference between our <term>Tincture</term> and <term>Multiplication</term>, and neither can happen without the other. For our <term>Stone</term>

<note>These symbols represent Mercury, the Sun (Gold), and Jupiter (Tin), signifying the planetary and metallic associations of the work.</note>

It does not <term>tinge</term><note>From the Latin 'tingere'; to transform or dye the metal into gold or silver.</note> unless it is first tinged itself; but when it is tinged, its quantity is multiplied. And when our Stone is multiplied, whether it be white or red, it also tinges.

Dear son, drive away from yourself the darkening of your understanding, so that you may be able to understand the **Secret of Secrets** <note>original: "Arcanum arcanorum"</note> that we have hidden with clever diligence in our books, which here comes out from the deeply hidden into the manifest light.

Hear and understand that our multiplication is nothing other than a repetition of our initial composed work. For in the first repetition, one part of our Stone tinges 3 parts of an imperfect body, and in so many parts it increases, and is multiplied in quantity.

In the second repetition, 1 part tinges 7 parts. In the third, 1 part tinges 15 parts.

In the fourth, 1 part tinges 31 parts. In the fifth, 63 parts. In the sixth, 127, and thus it is always multiplied and increases by so many parts even without end. If it were repeated 24 times, then 1 part would tinge twenty thousand times a thousand, four hundred forty-six thousand, four hundred thirty-one parts, which make 204 tons of gold.

See, my son, thus we have opened and explained to you our books—which until now have been locked with parabolic speech—against the commandment of the <term>Philosophers</term><note>original: "Philosophorum"; referring to the ancient alchemical masters who swore to keep the art secret.</note>.

<summary>This page explains the mathematical progression of 'multiplication,' where repeating the alchemical process exponentially increases the Stone's power to transform base metals into gold. The author claims to be revealing the 'Secret of Secrets' that was previously hidden in allegorical language.</summary>
<keywords>multiplication, Tincture, Secret of Secrets, Philosopher's Stone, transmutation, gold, alchemical repetition</keywords>

We hope, however, to be excused from their threats, so that we do not fall into their curse through the providence of God <note>original: "Verhängniß GOttes"; refers to the divine decree or destiny that governs the success of the alchemical work</note>.

To the Almighty God in Heaven, in whose power all things reside, we commend the secrets of this our little book; He who gives all art and all gifts to whom He will, and also withdraws them, and hides them within His divine power in such a way that the fools and the godless can by no means investigate or comprehend them.

Now you, son of wisdom <term>Sohn der Weißheit: a traditional alchemical address to a dedicated student or initiate</term>, you shall give thanks to God, who through His divine enlightenment <note>original: "Erleuchtung"</note> opens and closes the human understanding: May His name be blessed for all eternity,

->Amen.<-

->☿ ) o ( ☉<-

it does not tinge <note>original: "tingiret"; to dye or transform the color and nature of a metal</note> unless it is first tinged itself; but when it is tinged, its quantity is multiplied. And when our Stone is multiplied, whether it be white or red, it is also tinged.

Dear son, drive away from yourself the darkening of your understanding, so that you may be able to understand the <term>Secret of Secrets</term> <note>original Latin: "Arcanum arcanorum"</note>, which we have hidden with clever diligence in our books, and which here comes out from the deeply hidden into the manifest light.

Hear and understand that our multiplication is nothing other than a repetition of our initial composed work. For in the first repetition, one part of our Stone tinges 3 parts of an imperfect body, and in so many parts it increases, and is multiplied in quantity.

In the second repetition, 1 part tinges 7 parts. In the third, 1 part tinges 15 parts.

In the fourth, 1 part tinges 31 parts. In the fifth, 63 parts. In the sixth, 127, and thus it is always multiplied and increases by so many parts without end. If it were repeated 24 times, then 1 part would tinge twenty thousand times a thousand, four hundred forty-six thousand, four hundred thirty-one parts, which make 204 tons of gold.

See, my son, thus we have opened and explained to you our books—which until now have been locked with parabolic speeches—against the commandment of the <term>Philosophers</term> <note>original Latin: "Philosophorum"; referring here to the ancient alchemists</note>.

## A Brief Preface.

Next to the invocation of the name of Christ, I have considered that in the works of Nature—which are to be fashioned through the aid of Art—most practitioners understand the books of the **Philosophers** <note>original: "Philosophorum"; referring here to the ancient alchemists and natural philosophers</note> only according to the external shells of their words. Thus, they labor uselessly, and thereby fall into great damage of their property and goods.

Therefore, in this treatise, I wish to set forth a model and partly explain the Art, just as I found it in a book which was an explanation of **Aristotle's** writings on those things which arise in the air <note>original: "derer Dinge, so in der Luft entstehen"; likely referring to Aristotle's *Meteorologica*</note>. This book he sent in quiet peace to **Alexander** <note>Alexander the Great</note>, and it begins thus: *Take the animal and vegetable stone, etc.* <note>original: "Nimm den animalischen, und vegetabilischen Stein"</note>. Pope Honorius had this book translated from Hebrew into Latin by a Greek who knew the Art perfectly.

Hence, he passes by the various works of all metals and **spirits** <note>original: "Spirituum"; in alchemy, volatile substances like mercury or sulfur</note>, because Nature brings her degrees to the final, perfect end; and he indicates what is then to be done if one should be led deep into error and deceived by the metals through seemingly clever subtleties.

<summary>The author introduces a treatise based on a pseudo-Aristotelian text translated under Pope Honorius, warning that many alchemists fail by taking philosophical texts too literally. It establishes the work's lineage from Hebrew and Greek sources concerning the "animal and vegetable stone."</summary>
<keywords>Alchemy, Aristotle, Alexander the Great, Pope Honorius, Natural Philosophy, Transmutation, Hermetic Tradition</keywords>

Listen, my son, listen to the teaching of the father of <term>Philosophers</term> <note>original: "Philosophorum"</note>, <term>Aristotle</term> the <term>Spagyrist</term> <note>A "Spagyrist" is an alchemist who practices the art of separating and recombining natural substances to create medicines or transmutations.</note>, who commands that you take the animal, vegetable, and mineral stone, etc. From this it is evident that the stone is animalistic, which, like a serpent, is released, begotten, and falls down divided from the corruption of perfect human nature with diligence between two mountains; it is then enclosed in the pit of its cave, governed with moderate heat, established, and preserved.

This dragon is of a warm nature and seeks an exit before birth; it wishes to spoil its fruit and desires a miscarriage. Through all too violent heat or cold it is corrupted; especially in far too great a heat it must suffocate, and that which is sought can nevermore become of it. The poison of the dragon, however, goes through the veins, which is entirely taken away from it in the final vessel. And just as the subtle nature in the aforementioned veins was the cause of its growth and life, and brought it to a perfect nature, so also shall this be your intended goal and the first step toward perfection.

In every natural birth of a perfect nature, a convenient time of 40 days is given. Because now, through the joining and revolution of the 7 planets through the circle of the signs <note>The Zodiac</note>, and through the change of the 4 elements, everything contained under the <term>Circle</term> <note>original: "Circulo"</note> of the moon is correctly changed and recognized, so also in this work through

Through the steaming out, evaporation, ascending, descending, and extraction of the aforementioned four elements <note>The four classical elements: Earth, Water, Air, and Fire</note>, the four are finally converted into one. Just as a meadow is adorned with flowers by the dew of heaven, which receive their colors, scent, and various properties from that very same earth—the cause of which is the natural governance of the super-celestial bodies, which receive their form and effect within the earth—and this occurs because of the blackness of the Earth, the whiteness of the Water, the greenness of the Air, the redness of the Fire, and the mingling of nature.

The cause is that in the true conception of our Dragon <note>The "Dragon" is a common alchemical symbol for the prime matter or the volatile mercury undergoing transformation</note>, all perfect as well as mixed colors will appear. And this Dragon begins to look wondrous, and like a very black toad <note>The "Toad" represents the stage of *nigredo* or blackness, signifying the putrefaction of the matter</note> it becomes thick; and because of its lowliness and total dissolution, it desires to be released from its sadness: It raises and spreads its white wings, and begs that they may be cut off from it entirely and without great effort. This can happen when, by that very same governance of heat, its animated sweat <note>original: "beseelter Schweiß"; refers to the vapor or spirit rising from the heated matter</note>—which is Air and Water—and the Water alone is extracted moderately and slowly. And for this reason, the purification of the sweat should not be repeated over the earth, or for the sake of the earth; rather, one should properly remove the entrails and leave the head and feet lying very black in the vessel. Everything together shall be well-preserved in the heat under the aforementioned—

It becomes ash-colored (understand this is because it is dying) because the Earth almost begins to <term>fever</term><note>original: "febricitiren"; a term used to describe the intense internal heat of the matter during the alchemical process</note> due to its unnatural and accidental heat, and its heavy labor. Therefore, it is now time that one must moisten it, so that it can be strengthened and increased for our purpose. And between these two works, a delay of three days occurs, which is called by <note>Aristotle; though often cited in alchemical texts, these attributions were frequently to "Pseudo-Aristotle"</note> the time of the first <term>calcination</term><note>the process of reducing a substance to powder through heat</note>, and due to the lack of moisture, a wonderful, rational desire is caused or excited, because it now desires and seeks nothing else than that nature becomes strong and powerful. And then a natural union and desire begins among the natures, so that nature annihilates within itself that which is contrary to it, without the resistance of the elements.

     This **Dragon's Stomach** digests contrary things, and yet accepts nothing foreign, but only its own, and takes only as much as it can digest. For nature often completes her work not in abundance, but in the distinction of time. For in the first three changes, it should be dried. But on the twentieth day, the Earth is still powerful; in the first three changes, the Earth should therefore be moistened with its water up to the third part, until it is nourished to its perfect whiteness, which is not only dry but also completely and utterly no longer volatile.

Now when one possesses these beginnings, the holy Apostle Paul gives us counsel and teaching, where he speaks:
> Let no one deceive you through vain wisdom, etc. <note>A reference to Colossians 2:8, warning against worldly philosophy that lacks spiritual truth.</note>

One has no need for more than one thing, one vessel: since it is one medicine, and one governance—not indeed of the hands, but the effect of Nature, and a bringing together into One.

For this reason, protect the moderate heat from the cold, and from every foreign thing.

The first work is accomplished through natural <term>solution</term><note>The process of dissolving a solid into a liquid state</note>, <term>distillation</term>, <term>conjunction</term><note>The chemical union of substances</note>, and <term>calcination</term><note>Reducing a substance to powder through heat</note>, as well as the composition of its water, in a round-spherical vessel that is sealed on all sides and made of suitable material.

Then you have correctly achieved the work, insofar as the rules set forth above are sufficient for the worker. But whatever is less or more than this is of evil, and that which is sought cannot come from it.

The second work of our <term>dragon</term><note>A common alchemical symbol for primordial matter or mercury</note>, however, is that it be moistened—after it has reached the degree of whiteness—with two-thirds of its sweat and milk; for it is necessary that the earth becomes water, and if it is properly governed, it can be accomplished in a short time. Accordingly, moisten it on the seventh day, so that it appears to you at the top like a shining and bright mirror; there one will see the dragon in the vessel shed its feathers, and that its body is dissolved in the water at the sides. And this is the sign of the first perfection: the dissolution in water, and it is the completion of the work. Thus shall you continue until the end of the work.

This is now the moist <term>Luna</term><note>Latin for Moon; here referring to the silver stage of the process</note>, whose light is from the Sun, and through greater cooking is transformed into the same.

This is the silver that can be transformed into gold.

This is the leaven <note>original: "Sauerteig"; the ferment that transforms the base matter</note>, the rennet of the leaven: This is the frozen sulfur, dense and incombustible, and is the *Mercurius Alkebrith* <note>An Arabic-derived term for "Sulfur of the Sages"</note>, which is the white arsenic; thus one single thing has received countless names from the Wise.

For as many colors as there are in the first putrefaction <note>The "nigredo" or blackening stage where the matter decays to be reborn</note>, so many powers and effects it possesses. And more names are given to it because of its majesty, since words are merely signs pointing primarily to one thing, as Aristotle recounted many such names to Alexander in the aforementioned teachings on the Philosopher's Stone.

Therefore, let the leaves fall away and gather the fruits, for the fruit of the tree has led us into and through the dark and the hidden.

But this is the way: walk upon it, and turn neither to the right nor to the left, and you shall find both art and favor. And you shall find that Nature works nothing in vain or to no purpose. But whatever shall be above Nature, or outside of Nature, or against Nature, or against the manner and ways or movement of Nature, that cannot be brought to perfection. Or likewise, if something within her were stifled, suppressed, and destroyed, or were composed poorly and not in the proper manner, you would immediately fall into error, and the entire work would come to nothing.

<meta>This page continues the alchemical instructions regarding the "Dragon" (mercury or the base matter) and its purification through distillation. The previous page likely introduced the initial preparation of this substance.</meta>

The laboratory workers are led astray and mocked, yet in this error there is this consolation: that a portion of the water can be drawn away from it evenly through "vomiting"—that is, through distillation—and that which it has vomited forth shall be given back to it twice over, provided that the heat is maintained steadily and continuously, with the omission of two days. And this is the true, proper <term>Theriac</term> <note>A historical "universal antidote" or wonder-drug; here used metaphorically for the chemical agent that heals or stabilizes the matter</note> of the aforementioned Dragon, which cures it of all exhaustion. Now, where the Dragon allows itself to be easily dissolved, you may calculate the proportion of its water against it as double; and should it be too much, then cure its weakness as mentioned above.

But where the color of the Dragon changes—which is the second error in this work—let it stand longer with its water without the <term>Rectification</term> <note>The process of purifying a liquid by repeated distillation</note> of its earth, until the earth begins to shine like fish-eyes. And then moisten it as before, and do not cease until it becomes entirely a crystalline water, clear and without impurity; and from now on, at the end, it shall no longer be fed nor stopped up, but shall be preserved in its vessel from all uncleanness.

The color of this heavenly water, however, will be like the color of the firmament <note>The sky or heavens; implying a brilliant blue or ethereal clarity</note>. Its scent will be like the scent of a precious ointment of spices. But beware that you do not drink of it, because of its penetrating and burning power, except only after its <term>Projection</term> <note>The final stage of the Great Work where the alchemical agent is cast upon base metals to transform them</note>, when it has been applied to others, as I will report at the end.
